B^{*}&0&I_{d}&C_{0}&{\bf e}_{d,x}\\
0&{\bf e}^{*}_{d,r}&0&0&0\end{array}\right)\right|

Expanding along the first column this simplifies further to

\displaystyle|\det S_{1}|=

\displaystyle|\det B|^{2}\cdot|\det B^{*}|\cdot\left|\det\left(\begin{array}[]{ccccc}0&0&-M_{n-1}^{UL}(1)&B-M_{n-1}^{UR}(1)&0\\
I_{d}&0&-M_{n-1}^{LL}(1)&-M_{n-1}^{LR}(1)&0\\
0&B&-M_{\ell-1}^{UL}(1)&-M_{\ell-1}^{UR}(1)&0\\
I_{d}&0&(B^{*})^{-1}&(B^{*})^{-1}C_{0}&(B^{*})^{-1}{\bf e}_{d,x}\\
0&{\bf e}^{*}_{d,r}&0&0&0\end{array}\right)\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|\det B|^{3}\cdot\left|\det\left(\begin{array}[]{cccccc}0&0&0&-M_{n-1}^{UL}(1)&B-M_{n-1}^{UR}(1)&0\\
0&I_{d}&0&-M_{n-1}^{LL}(1)&-M_{n-1}^{LR}(1)&0\\
0&0&B&-M_{\ell-1}^{UL}(1)&-M_{\ell-1}^{UR}(1)&0\\
I_{d}&0&0&0&-B&0\\
0&I_{d}&0&(B^{*})^{-1}&(B^{*})^{-1}C_{0}&(B^{*})^{-1}{\bf e}_{d,x}\\
0&0&{\bf e}^{*}_{d,r}&0&0&0\end{array}\right)\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|\det B|^{3}\cdot\left|\det\left(\begin{array}[]{c|c|c|c}\left(\begin{matrix}0&0\\
0&I_{d}\end{matrix}\right)&&\left(\begin{matrix}0&B\\
0&0\end{matrix}\right)-M_{n-1}(1)&\\
\hline\cr&B&-M_{\ell-1}^{U}(1)&\\
\hline\cr I_{2d}&&-M_{1}^{-1}(0)&\left(\begin{matrix}0\\
I_{d}\end{matrix}\right)(B^{*})^{-1}{\bf e}_{d,x}\\
\hline\cr&{\bf e}^{*}_{d,r}&&\end{array}\right)\right|

(4.256)

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle|\det B|^{3}\cdot|\det S_{3}|,

in which
M_{\ell-1}^{U}(1)=(M_{\ell-1}^{UL}(1),M_{\ell-1}^{UR}(1))=(I_{d},0)\cdot M_{\ell-1}(1)
. Empty spaces are to be filled in by zeros.
We carry out the following row operations on
S_{3}
that do not alter the determinant of
S_{3}
. Below
\text{row}_{j}
refers to the
j
-th (block-valued) row of
S_{3}
. Replacing

\text{row}_{1}\longrightarrow\text{row}_{1}-\left(\begin{matrix}0&0\\
0&I_{d}\end{matrix}\right)\cdot\text{row}_{3},

and

\text{row}_{4}\longrightarrow\text{row}_{4}-{\bf e}_{d,r}^{*}B^{-1}\cdot\text{row}_{2},

we arrive at

\displaystyle S_{3}^{(1)}=\left(\begin{array}[]{c|c|c|c}&&\left(\begin{matrix}0&B\\
0&0\end{matrix}\right)-M_{n-1}(1)+\left(\begin{matrix}0&0\\
0&I_{d}\end{matrix}\right)M_{1}^{-1}(0)&-\left(\begin{matrix}0\\
I_{d}\end{matrix}\right)(B^{*})^{-1}{\bf e}_{d,x}\\
\hline\cr&B&-(I_{d},0)\cdot M_{\ell-1}(1)&\\
\hline\cr I_{2d}&&-M_{1}^{-1}(0)&\left(\begin{matrix}0\\
I_{d}\end{matrix}\right)(B^{*})^{-1}{\bf e}_{d,x}\\
\hline\cr&&{\bf e}_{d,r}^{*}B^{-1}(I_{d},0)\cdot M_{\ell-1}(1)&\end{array}\right).

Next, multiplying
\text{col}_{3}
of
S_{3}^{(1)}
on the right by
M_{1}(0)
(for which
\det M_{1}(0)=1
, so
\det S_{3}^{(1)}
is invariant), yields

\displaystyle S_{3}^{(2)}=\left(\begin{array}[]{c|c|c|c}&&\left(\begin{matrix}0&B\\
0&0\end{matrix}\right)M_{1}(0)-M_{n}(0)+\left(\begin{matrix}0&0\\
0&I_{d}\end{matrix}\right)&-\left(\begin{matrix}0\\
I_{d}\end{matrix}\right)(B^{*})^{-1}{\bf e}_{d,x}\\
\hline\cr&B&-(I_{d},0)\cdot M_{\ell}(0)&\\
\hline\cr I_{2d}&&-I_{d}&\left(\begin{matrix}0\\
I_{d}\end{matrix}\right)(B^{*})^{-1}{\bf e}_{d,x}\\
\hline\cr&&{\bf e}_{d,r}^{*}B^{-1}(I_{d},0)\cdot M_{\ell}(0)&\end{array}\right).

Expanding the determinant along the first two columns, and noting that
\left(\begin{matrix}0&B\\
0&0\end{matrix}\right)M_{1}(0)=\left(\begin{matrix}I_{d}&0\\
0&0\end{matrix}\right)
, yields

\displaystyle|\det S_{3}^{(2)}|

\displaystyle=|\det B|\cdot\left|\det\left(\begin{array}[]{c|c}-M_{n}(0)+I_{2d}&-\left(\begin{matrix}0\\
I_{d}\end{matrix}\right)(B^{*})^{-1}{\bf e}_{d,x}\\
\hline\cr{\bf e}_{d,r}^{*}B^{-1}(I_{d},0)\cdot M_{\ell}(0)&0\end{array}\right)\right|

(4.267)

\displaystyle=:|\det B|\cdot|\det S_{3}^{(3)}|.

We expand
S_{3}^{(3)}
as the following product:

\displaystyle S_{3}^{(3)}=

\displaystyle\left(\begin{array}[]{c|c}I_{2d}&\\
\hline\cr&{\bf e}_{d,r}^{*}B^{-1}(I_{d},0)\end{array}\right)_{(2d+1)\times 4d}\cdot\left(\begin{array}[]{c|c}-M_{n-\ell}(\ell)&I_{2d}\\
\hline\cr I_{2d}&\end{array}\right)\cdot\left(\begin{array}[]{c|c}M_{\ell}(0)&\\
\hline\cr&I_{2d}\end{array}\right)\cdot

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\left(\begin{array}[]{c|c}I_{2d}&\\
\hline\cr I_{2d}&-\left(\begin{matrix}0\\
I_{d}\end{matrix}\right)(B^{*})^{-1}{\bf e}_{d,x}\end{array}\right)_{4d\times(2d+1)}

We bound

\displaystyle|\det S_{3}^{(3)}|=

\displaystyle\left|\left(\wedge^{2d+1}\left(\begin{array}[]{c|c}I_{2d}&\\
\hline\cr&{\bf e}_{d,r}^{*}B^{-1}(I_{d},0)\end{array}\right)\right)\cdot\left(\wedge^{2d+1}\left(\begin{array}[]{c|c}-M_{n-\ell}(\ell)&I_{2d}\\
\hline\cr I_{2d}&\end{array}\right)\right)\cdot\right.

\displaystyle\left.\qquad\cdot\left(\wedge^{2d+1}\left(\begin{array}[]{c|c}M_{\ell}(0)&\\
\hline\cr&I_{2d}\end{array}\right)\right)\cdot\left(\wedge^{2d+1}\left(\begin{array}[]{c|c}I_{2d}&\\
\hline\cr I_{2d}&-\left(\begin{matrix}0\\
I_{d}\end{matrix}\right)(B^{*})^{-1}{\bf e}_{d,x}\end{array}\right)\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{y=2d+1}^{3d}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq j_{1}<...<j_{2d+1}\leq 4d\\
1\leq k_{1}<...<k_{2d+1}\leq 4d\\
1\leq\ell_{1}<...<\ell_{2d+1}\leq 4d\end{subarray}}\left|\det\left(\begin{array}[]{c|c}I_{2d}&\\
\hline\cr&{\bf e}_{d,r}^{*}B^{-1}(I_{d},0)\end{array}\right)_{(1,...,2d,2d+1),(1,...,2d,y)}\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\det\left|\left(\begin{array}[]{c|c}-M_{n-\ell}(\ell)&I_{2d}\\
\hline\cr I_{2d}&\end{array}\right)_{(1,...,2d,y),(j_{1},...,j_{2d+1})}\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\left|\det\left(\begin{array}[]{c|c}M_{\ell}(0)&\\
\hline\cr&I_{2d}\end{array}\right)_{(j_{1},...,j_{2d+1}),(k_{1},...,k_{2d+1})}\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\left|\det\left(\begin{array}[]{c|c}I_{2d}&\\
\hline\cr I_{2d}&-\left(\begin{matrix}0\\
I_{d}\end{matrix}\right)(B^{*})^{-1}{\bf e}_{d,x}\end{array}\right)_{(k_{1},...,k_{2d+1}),(\ell_{1},...,\ell_{2d+1})}\right|.

Suppose that

k_{1}<...<k_{m_{0}}\leq 2d<k_{m_{0}+1}<...<k_{2d+1}.

In order for

\displaystyle\det\left(\begin{array}[]{c|c}M_{\ell}(0)&\\
\hline\cr&I_{2d}\end{array}\right)_{(j_{1},...,j_{2d+1}),(k_{1},...,k_{2d+1})},

to be non-vanishing, necessarily

(4.294)

\displaystyle j_{1}<...<j_{m_{0}}\leq 2d\text{\ \ and\ \ }j_{m}=k_{m}\text{ for }m_{0}+1\leq m\leq 2d+1,

If it is non-vanishing, then

\displaystyle\left|\det\left(\begin{array}[]{c|c}M_{\ell}(0)&\\
\hline\cr&I_{2d}\end{array}\right)_{(j_{1},...,j_{2d+1}),(k_{1},...,k_{2d+1})}\right|

(4.297)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\det(M_{\ell}(0))_{\{j_{1},...,j_{m_{0}}\},\{k_{1},...,k_{m_{0}}\}}\right|

(4.298)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\wedge^{m_{0}}M_{\ell}(0)\|\leq e^{\ell(1+\varepsilon)\cdot\hat{L}^{m_{0}}},

where we used Lemma
2.1
to bound
\|\wedge^{m_{0}}M_{\ell}(0)\|
.
Taking (
4.294
) into account, one has

\displaystyle\left|\det\left(\begin{array}[]{c|c}-M_{n-\ell}(\ell)&I_{2d}\\
\hline\cr I_{2d}&\end{array}\right)_{(1,...,2d,y),(j_{1},...,j_{2d+1})}\right|

(4.301)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\det(M_{n-\ell}(\ell))_{\{1,...,2d\}\setminus\{j_{m_{0}+1}-2d,...,j_{2d+1}-2d\},\{j_{1},...,j_{m_{0}}\}\setminus\{y-2d\}}\right|

(4.302)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\wedge^{m_{0}-1}M_{n-\ell}(\ell)\|\leq e^{(n-\ell)(1+\varepsilon)\cdot\hat{L}^{m_{0}-1}},

where we used Lemma
2.1
again.
The following estimates are easy to verify:

\displaystyle\left|\det\left(\begin{array}[]{c|c}I_{2d}&\\
\hline\cr&{\bf e}_{d,r}^{*}B^{-1}(I_{d},0)\end{array}\right)_{(1,...,2d,2d+1),(1,...,2d,y)}\right|\leq C\|B^{-1}\|,\text{ and }

\displaystyle\left|\det\left(\begin{array}[]{c|c}I_{2d}&\\
\hline\cr I_{2d}&-\left(\begin{matrix}0\\
I_{d}\end{matrix}\right)(B^{*})^{-1}{\bf e}_{d,x}\end{array}\right)_{(k_{1},...,k_{2d+1}),(\ell_{1},...,\ell_{2d+1})}\right|\leq C\|B^{-1}\|.

Combining (
4
), (
4
) with (
4
), one has

(4.307)

\displaystyle|\det S_{3}^{(3)}|\leq

\displaystyle C_{d}\|B^{-1}\|^{2}\sum_{m_{0}}e^{\ell(1+\varepsilon)\cdot\hat{L}^{m_{0}}+(n-\ell)(1+\varepsilon)\cdot\hat{L}^{m_{0}-1}}

(4.308)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{d}\|B^{-1}\|^{2}\max\left(e^{\ell(1+\varepsilon)\cdot\hat{L}^{d}+(n-\ell)(1+\varepsilon)\cdot\hat{L}^{d-1}},e^{\ell(1+\varepsilon)\cdot\hat{L}^{d+1}+(n-\ell)(1+\varepsilon)\cdot\hat{L}^{d}}\right),

where we used that
\hat{L}^{1}<...<\hat{L}^{d-1}<\hat{L}^{d}
and
\hat{L}^{d}>\hat{L}^{d+1}>...>\hat{L}^{2d}
, due to
\hat{L}_{d}>0>\hat{L}_{d+1}
.
Combining the above with (
4.230
),(
4
) and (
4
) yields the claimed result for Case 1.

Case 2
. Suppose
(n-1)d\leq x\leq nd-1
.
Again, we write
y=\ell d+r
with
3\leq\ell\leq n-2
and
0\leq r\leq d-1
. We also denote
x-(n-1)d=\tilde{x}
.
In analogy to (
4
), we have
|\mu_{n,x,y}(\theta)|=|\det\tilde{R}_{x,y}|
, where

\displaystyle\qquad\tilde{R}_{x,y}=

\displaystyle\left(\begin{array}[]{c|c|c|c|c|c|c|c|c|c}C_{n-1}&B^{*}&&&&&&&B&{\bf e}_{d,\tilde{x}}\\
\hline\cr B&\ddots&\ddots&&&&&&&\\
\hline\cr&\ddots&\ddots&\ddots&&&&&&\\
\hline\cr&&\ddots&\ddots&B^{*}&&&&&\\
\hline\cr&&&B&C_{\ell}&B^{*}&&&&\\
\hline\cr&&&&B&C_{\ell-1}&\ddots&&&\\
\hline\cr&&&&&\ddots&\ddots&\ddots&&\\
\hline\cr&&&&&&\ddots&\ddots&B^{*}&\\
\hline\cr B^{*}&&&&&&&B&C_{0}&\\
\hline\cr&&&&{\bf e}_{d,r}^{*}&&&&&\end{array}\right)

Applying exactly the same row operations on
\text{Row}_{1}
,
\text{Row}_{2}
and
\text{Row}_{n-\ell+1}
as in Case 1, reduces
\tilde{R}_{x,y}
to the following shape:

\displaystyle\tilde{R}^{(1)}_{x,y}=\left(\begin{array}[]{c|c|c|c|c|c|c|c|c|c|c|c|c}0&0&0&\cdots&&&&&\cdots&0&-M_{n-1}^{UL}(1)B&B-M_{n-1}^{UR}(1)&{\bf e}_{d,\tilde{x}}\\
\hline\cr B&0&0&\cdots&&&&&\cdots&0&-M_{n-2}^{UL}(1)B&-M_{n-2}^{UR}(1)&0\\
\hline\cr 0&B&C_{n-3}&B^{*}&&&&&&&&&\vdots\\
\hline\cr\vdots&&\ddots&\ddots&\ddots&&&&&&&&\\
\hline\cr&&&\ddots&\ddots&\ddots&&&&&&&\\
\hline\cr\vdots&&&&B&C_{\ell}&B^{*}&&&&&&\vdots\\
\hline\cr 0&\cdots&&\cdots&0&B&0&0&\cdots&0&-M_{\ell-1}^{UL}(1)B&-M_{\ell-1}^{UR}(1)&0\\
\hline\cr\vdots&&&&&0&B&C_{\ell-2}&B^{*}&&&0&0\\
\hline\cr&&&&&&&\ddots&\ddots&\ddots&&\vdots&\vdots\\
\hline\cr\vdots&&&&&&&&\ddots&\ddots&\ddots&0&\\
\hline\cr 0&&&&&&&&&\ddots&\ddots&B^{*}&\vdots\\
\hline\cr B^{*}&0&\cdots&&&&&&\cdots&0&B&C_{0}&0\\
\hline\cr 0&\cdots&&\cdots&0&{\bf e}_{d,r}^{*}&0&\cdots&&\cdots&0&0&0\end{array}\right)

This implies, similarly to (
4.230
), that

(4.332)

\displaystyle|\det\tilde{R}_{x,y}|=|\det\tilde{R}^{(1)}_{x,y}|=|\det B|^{n-4}\cdot|\det\tilde{S}_{1}|,

where in analogy with (
4
) and (
4
) one has

\displaystyle|\det\tilde{S}_{1}|=

\displaystyle\left|\det\left(\begin{array}[]{ccccc}0&0&-M_{n-1}^{UL}(1)B&B-M_{n-1}^{UR}(1)&{\bf e}_{d,\tilde{x}}\\
B&0&-BM_{n-1}^{LL}(1)B&-BM_{n-1}^{LR}(1)&0\\
0&B&-M_{\ell-1}^{UL}(1)B&-M_{\ell-1}^{UR}(1)&0\\
B^{*}&0&B&C_{0}&0\\
0&{\bf e}^{*}_{d,r}&0&0&0\end{array}\right)\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|\det B|\cdot\left|\det\left(\begin{array}[]{ccccc}0&0&-M_{n-1}^{UL}(1)&B-M_{n-1}^{UR}(1)&{\bf e}_{d,\tilde{x}}\\
B&0&-BM_{n-1}^{LL}(1)&-BM_{n-1}^{LR}(1)&0\\
0&B&-M_{\ell-1}^{UL}(1)&-M_{\ell-1}^{UR}(1)&0\\
B^{*}&0&I_{d}&C_{0}&0\\
0&{\bf e}^{*}_{d,r}&0&0&0\end{array}\right)\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|\det B|^{2}\cdot|\det B^{*}|\cdot\left|\det\left(\begin{array}[]{ccccc}0&0&-M_{n-1}^{UL}(1)&B-M_{n-1}^{UR}(1)&{\bf e}_{d,\tilde{x}}\\
I_{d}&0&-M_{n-1}^{LL}(1)&-M_{n-1}^{LR}(1)&0\\
0&B&-M_{\ell-1}^{UL}(1)&-M_{\ell-1}^{UR}(1)&0\\
I_{d}&0&(B^{*})^{-1}&(B^{*})^{-1}C_{0}&0\\
0&{\bf e}^{*}_{d,r}&0&0&0\end{array}\right)\right|

Rewriting this in block form yields

\displaystyle|\det\tilde{S}_{1}|=

\displaystyle|\det B|^{3}\cdot\left|\det\left(\begin{array}[]{c|c|c|c}\left(\begin{matrix}0&0\\
0&I_{d}\end{matrix}\right)&&\left(\begin{matrix}0&B\\
0&0\end{matrix}\right)-M_{n-1}(1)&\left(\begin{matrix}I_{d}\\
0\end{matrix}\right){\bf e}_{d,\tilde{x}}\\
\hline\cr&B&-M_{\ell-1}^{U}(1)&\\
\hline\cr I_{2d}&&-M_{1}^{-1}(0)&\\
\hline\cr&{\bf e}^{*}_{d,r}&&\end{array}\right)\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|\det B|^{3}\cdot\left|\det\left(\begin{array}[]{c|c|c|c}\left(\begin{matrix}0&0\\
0&I_{d}\end{matrix}\right)&&\left(\begin{matrix}0&B\\
0&0\end{matrix}\right)M_{1}(0)-M_{n}(0)&\left(\begin{matrix}I_{d}\\
0\end{matrix}\right){\bf e}_{d,\tilde{x}}\\
\hline\cr&B&-(I_{d},0)\,M_{\ell}(0)&\\
\hline\cr I_{2d}&&-I_{2d}&\\
\hline\cr&{\bf e}^{*}_{d,r}&&\end{array}\right)\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|\det B|^{3}\cdot\left|\det\left(\begin{array}[]{c|c|c|c}&&I_{2d}-M_{n}(0)&\left(\begin{matrix}I_{d}\\
0\end{matrix}\right){\bf e}_{d,\tilde{x}}\\
\hline\cr&B&-(I_{d},0)\,M_{\ell}(0)&\\
\hline\cr I_{2d}&&-I_{2d}&\\
\hline\cr&{\bf e}^{*}_{d,r}&&\end{array}\right)\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|\det B|^{3}\cdot\left|\det\left(\begin{array}[]{c|c|c|c}&&I_{2d}-M_{n}(0)&\left(\begin{matrix}I_{d}\\
0\end{matrix}\right){\bf e}_{d,\tilde{x}}\\
\hline\cr&B&-(I_{d},0)\,M_{\ell}(0)&\\
\hline\cr I_{2d}&&-I_{2d}&\\
\hline\cr&&{\bf e}_{d,r}^{*}B^{-1}(I_{d},0)\,M_{\ell}(0)&\end{array}\right)\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|\det B|^{3}\cdot\left|\det\left(\begin{array}[]{c|c|c|c}&&I_{2d}-M_{n}(0)&\left(\begin{matrix}I_{d}\\
0\end{matrix}\right){\bf e}_{d,\tilde{x}}\\
\hline\cr&B&-(I_{d},0)\,M_{\ell}(0)&\\
\hline\cr I_{2d}&&-I_{2d}&\\
\hline\cr&{\bf e}^{*}_{d,r}&&\end{array}\right)\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|\det B|^{3}\cdot\left|\det\left(\begin{array}[]{c|c}I_{2d}-M_{n}(0)&\left(\begin{matrix}I_{d}\\
0\end{matrix}\right){\bf e}_{d,\tilde{x}}\\
\hline\cr{\bf e}_{d,r}^{*}B^{-1}(I_{d},0)\,M_{\ell}(0)&\end{array}\right)\right|

(4.370)

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle|\det B|^{4}\cdot|\det\tilde{S}_{(3)}|,

where

\displaystyle\tilde{S}_{3}^{(3)}=

\displaystyle\left(\begin{array}[]{c|c}I_{2d}&\\
\hline\cr&{\bf e}_{d,r}^{*}B^{-1}(I_{d},0)\end{array}\right)_{(2d+1)\times 4d}\cdot\left(\begin{array}[]{c|c}-M_{n-\ell}(\ell)&I_{2d}\\
\hline\cr I_{2d}&\end{array}\right)\cdot\left(\begin{array}[]{c|c}M_{\ell}(0)&\\
\hline\cr&I_{2d}\end{array}\right)\cdot

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\left(\begin{array}[]{c|c}I_{2d}&\\
\hline\cr I_{2d}&-\left(\begin{matrix}I_{d}\\
0\end{matrix}\right){\bf e}_{d,\tilde{x}}\end{array}\right)_{4d\times(2d+1)}

The remainder of the argument is exactly the same as in Case 1. ∎

5.
Denominator lower bound for periodic boundary condition

In this section we prove Lemma
3.2
.
Throughout, we denote
B_{0}
by
B
, and
C_{0}(\theta+jd\alpha)-E
by
C_{j}
.
First, we relate the determinant
f_{E,n}(\theta)=\det(P_{\alpha,n}(\theta)-E)
to the transfer matrix
M_{n,E}
.

Lemma 5.1
.

The following identity holds pointwise in
\theta

(5.379)

\displaystyle|f_{E,n}(\theta)|=|\det B|^{n}\cdot|\det(M_{n,E}(\theta)-I_{2d})|.

Proof.

The proof is similar to, and is in fact simpler than, the one of Lemma
3.1
.
Indeed, following exactly the same row operations (that do not alter
f_{E,n}
) applied to
\text{Row}_{1}
,
\text{Row}_{2}
and
\text{Row}_{n-\ell+1}
of
P_{\alpha,n}(\theta)-E
, instead of those on
R_{x,y}
in Section
4
(note these operations do not involve the terms
{\bf e}_{d,x}
or
{\bf e}_{d,r}^{*}
), in analogy to (
4
) we arrive at the equality

\displaystyle P^{(1)}_{n}(\theta)=\left(\begin{array}[]{c|c|c|c|c|c|c|c|c|c|c|c}0&0&0&\cdots&&&&&\cdots&0&-M_{n-1}^{UL}(1)B&B-M_{n-1}^{UR}(1)\\
\hline\cr B&0&0&\cdots&&&&&\cdots&0&-M_{n-2}^{UL}(1)B&-M_{n-2}^{UR}(1)\\
\hline\cr 0&B&C_{n-3}&B^{*}&&&&&&&&\\
\hline\cr\vdots&&\ddots&\ddots&\ddots&&&&&&&\\
\hline\cr&&&\ddots&\ddots&\ddots&&&&&&\\
\hline\cr\vdots&&&&B&C_{\ell}&B^{*}&&&&&\\
\hline\cr 0&\cdots&&\cdots&0&B&0&0&\cdots&0&-M_{\ell-1}^{UL}(1)B&-M_{\ell-1}^{UR}(1)\\
\hline\cr\vdots&&&&&0&B&C_{\ell-2}&B^{*}&&&0\\
\hline\cr&&&&&&&\ddots&\ddots&\ddots&&\vdots\\
\hline\cr\vdots&&&&&&&&\ddots&\ddots&\ddots&0\\
\hline\cr 0&&&&&&&&&\ddots&\ddots&B^{*}\\
\hline\cr B^{*}&0&\cdots&&&&&&\cdots&0&B&C_{0}\end{array}\right)

This implies, similarly to (
4.230
), that

(5.392)

\displaystyle|f_{E,n}(\theta)|=|\det P_{n}^{(}1)(\theta)|=|\det B|^{n-4}\cdot|\det\tilde{S}_{1}|,

where

\displaystyle\tilde{S}_{1}:=\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}0&0&-M_{n-1}^{UL}(1)B&B-M_{n-1}^{UR}(1)\\
B&0&-M_{n-2}^{UL}(1)B&-M_{n-2}^{UR}(1)\\
0&B&-M_{\ell-1}^{UL}(1)B&-M_{\ell-1}^{UR}(1)\\
B^{*}&0&B&C_{0}\end{array}\right).

Furthermore, in place of (
4
), we now have

\displaystyle|\det\tilde{S}_{1}|=

\displaystyle|\det B|^{2}\cdot\left|\det\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}0&-M_{n-1}^{UL}(1)&B-M_{n-1}^{UR}(1)\\
B&-M_{n-2}^{UL}(1)&-M_{n-2}^{UR}(1)\\
B^{*}&I_{d}&C_{0}\end{array}\right)\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|\det B|^{2}\cdot|\det B^{*}|\cdot\left|\det\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}0&-M_{n-1}^{UL}(1)&B-M_{n-1}^{UR}(1)\\
B&-M_{n-2}^{UL}(1)&-M_{n-2}^{UR}(1)\\
I_{d}&(B^{*})^{-1}&(B^{*})^{-1}C_{0}\end{array}\right)\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|\det B|^{4}\cdot\left|\det\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}0&0&-M_{n-1}^{UL}(1)&B-M_{n-1}^{UR}(1)\\
0&I_{d}&-M_{n-1}^{LL}(1)&-M_{n-1}^{LR}(1)\\
I_{d}&0&0&-B\\
0&I_{d}&(B^{*})^{-1}&(B^{*})^{-1}C_{0}\end{array}\right)\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|\det B|^{4}\cdot\left|\det\left(\begin{array}[]{c|c}\left(\begin{matrix}0&0\\
0&I_{d}\end{matrix}\right)&\left(\begin{matrix}0&B\\
0&0\end{matrix}\right)-M_{n-1}(1)\\
\hline\cr I_{2d}&-M_{1}^{-1}(0)\end{array}\right)\right|

Rewriting this in block form yields

\displaystyle|\det\tilde{S}_{1}|=

\displaystyle|\det B|^{4}\cdot\left|\det\left(\begin{array}[]{c|c}\left(\begin{matrix}0&0\\
0&I_{d}\end{matrix}\right)&\left(\begin{matrix}0&B\\
0&0\end{matrix}\right)M_{1}(0)-M_{n}(0)\\
\hline\cr I_{2d}&-I_{2d}\end{array}\right)\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|\det B|^{4}\cdot\left|\det\left(\begin{array}[]{c|c}\left(\begin{matrix}0&0\\
0&I_{d}\end{matrix}\right)&I_{2d}-M_{n}(0)\\
\hline\cr I_{2d}&0\end{array}\right)\right|

(5.413)

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle|\det B|^{4}\cdot|\det M_{n}(0)-I_{2d}|.

The claimed result follows by combining (
5.392
) with (
5
).
∎

To estimate
\det(M_{n,E}(\theta)-I_{2d})
, we need the following elementary lemma concerning the vector spaces associated with the singular value decomposition of a symplectic matrix. We include the proof for the sake of completeness.

Lemma 5.2
.

Let
M\in\mathbb{C}^{2d\times 2d}
be a complex symplectic matrix, i.e.,
M^{*}\Omega M=\Omega
. Let
\sigma_{1}\geq...\geq\sigma_{2d}
be the singular values of
M
. Let
\mu_{1}>...>\mu_{k}
be the non-repeated singular values and
V_{j}
,
1\leq j\leq k
, be the singular vector space of
M
associated to
\mu_{j}
. Similarly, let
W_{j}
be the singular vector space of
M^{*}
associated to
\mu_{j}
. Then

(5.414)

\displaystyle\Omega V_{j}=V_{k+1-j},\text{ and }\Omega W_{j}=W_{k+1-j}.

Proof.

Let
v_{j}\in V_{j}
be a unit vector, and
w_{j}
be the unit vector such that

(5.415)

\displaystyle Mv_{j}=\mu_{j}w_{j}.

This implies

(5.416)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}M^{*}w_{j}=\mu_{j}v_{j}\\
(M^{*})^{-1}v_{j}=\mu_{j}^{-1}w_{j}\\
M^{-1}w_{j}=\mu_{j}^{-1}v_{j}\end{cases}

Since
M
is symplectic, we also have

(5.417)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}M^{*}\Omega M=\Omega\\
\Omega M^{*}\Omega=-M^{-1}\\
\Omega M\Omega=-(M^{*})^{-1}\end{cases}

Combining (
5.415
), (
5.416
) with (
5.417
), yields

(5.418)

\displaystyle M^{*}M\Omega v_{j}=-M^{*}\Omega^{2}M\Omega v_{j}=M^{*}\Omega(M^{*})^{-1}v_{j}=M^{*}\Omega\mu_{j}^{-1}w_{j}=

\displaystyle-\mu_{j}^{-1}\Omega^{2}M^{*}\Omega w_{j}

(5.419)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mu_{j}^{-1}\Omega M^{-1}w_{j}

(5.420)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mu_{j}^{-2}\Omega v_{j}.

This implies that
\Omega v_{j}
is an eigenvector of
M^{*}M
associated to the eigenvalue
\mu_{j}^{-2}=\mu_{k+1-j}^{2}
, whence
\Omega v_{j}\in V_{k+1-j}
.
Therefore,
\Omega V_{j}\subset V_{k+1-j}
for any
1\leq j\leq k
, which implies that
\Omega V_{j}=V_{k+1-j}
. The claim concerning
W_{j}
can be proved analogously.
∎

Next, we will apply the previous lemma to
M_{n,E}(\theta)
. For simplicity, we shall write
\hat{L}^{j}(d\alpha,M_{E})
,
\hat{L}_{j}(d\alpha,M_{E})
as
\hat{L}^{j}
and
\hat{L}_{j}
, respectively.
Recall that the large deviation sets
\mathcal{B}_{n,E,\varepsilon}^{d}
was defined in (
2.52
).
By Lemma
2.2
, this set is exponentially small for large enough
n
.
We fix
0<\varepsilon<\frac{\hat{L}_{d}}{11\hat{L}^{d}}
.
Note that for such
\varepsilon
,

(5.421)

\displaystyle(1-10\varepsilon)\hat{L}^{d}>(1+\varepsilon)\hat{L}^{d-1}

Let
\theta\in(\mathcal{B}^{d}_{n,E,\varepsilon})^{c}
,
and
v_{j,n}(\theta)
be the normalized singular vector of
M_{n,E}(\theta)
corresponding to the singular value
\sigma_{j}(M_{n,E}(\theta))
. Suppose
w_{j,n}(\theta)
is the normalized singular vector of
M_{n,E}^{*}(\theta)
such that

M_{n,E}(\theta)v_{j,n}(\theta)=\sigma_{j}(M_{n,E}(\theta))w_{j,n}(\theta).

According to Lemmas
2.1
and
2.2
, for
\theta\in(\mathcal{B}_{n,E,\varepsilon}^{d})^{c}
, one has

(5.422)

\displaystyle\prod_{j=1}^{d}\sigma_{j}(M_{n,E}(\theta))=\|\wedge^{d}M_{n,E}(\theta)\|\geq(1-\varepsilon)\cdot\hat{L}^{d}\geq(1+\varepsilon)\cdot\hat{L}^{d+1}\geq\prod_{j=1}^{d+1}\sigma_{j}(M_{n,E}(\theta)).

It follows that

\sigma_{d}(M_{n,E}(\theta))>1>\sigma_{d+1}(M_{n,E}(\theta))=(\sigma_{d}(M_{n,E}(\theta)))^{-1}.

Let
V_{j,n}(\theta)
and
W_{j,n}(\theta)
be as defined in Lemma
5.2
with
M
replaced with
M_{n,E}(\theta)
. Clearly
k
is even. By that lemma, for any
1\leq j\leq k/2
,

(5.423)

\displaystyle\Omega V_{j,n}(\theta)=V_{k+1-j,n}(\theta),\text{ and }\Omega W_{j,n}(\theta)=W_{k+1-j,n}(\theta).

This implies the following relations between the most expanding and most contracting
d
-planes:

(5.424)

\displaystyle(\wedge^{d}\Omega)(v_{1,n}(\theta)\wedge\cdots\wedge v_{d,n}(\theta))=\pm v_{d+1,n}(\theta)\wedge\cdots\wedge v_{2d,n}(\theta),\text{ and }

(5.425)

\displaystyle(\wedge^{d}\Omega)(w_{1,n}(\theta)\wedge\cdots\wedge w_{d,n}(\theta))=\pm w_{d+1,n}(\theta)\wedge\cdots\wedge w_{2d,n}(\theta),

and in particular

(5.426)

\displaystyle\left|\langle v_{1,n}(\theta)\wedge\cdots\wedge v_{d,n}(\theta),w_{1,n}(\theta)\wedge\cdots\wedge w_{d,n}(\theta)\rangle\right|

(5.427)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad=\left|\langle v_{d+1,n}(\theta)\wedge\cdots\wedge v_{2d,n}(\theta),w_{d+1,n}(\theta)\wedge\cdots\wedge w_{2d,n}(\theta)\rangle\right|

The following result will play a crucial role in our argument, cf.
[
GS3
]
*Lemma 3.2. It will enable us to show that for a subsequence of
n
, the most expanding
d
-planes of
M_{n,E}(\theta)
and
M_{n,E}^{*}(\theta)
are not entirely orthogonal.

Lemma 5.3
.

Assume that
\beta(\alpha)=0
. Then for any small
\varepsilon>0
, there exists
N_{0}=N_{0}(\varepsilon,\alpha,E,d)
and
\kappa_{0}=\kappa_{0}(\varepsilon,\alpha,E,d,N_{0})>0
such that for any
\kappa_{0}
-admissible
N_{1}
(those satisfying
\|N_{1}d\alpha\|_{\mathbb{T}}\leq\kappa_{0}
), and
N_{1}\geq 8N_{0}
, we have

(5.428)

\displaystyle\mathrm{mes}(\tilde{\mathcal{B}}^{d}_{N_{1},E,\varepsilon}):=\mathrm{mes}\Big(\left\{\theta:\,\left|\frac{1}{2N_{1}}\log\|\wedge^{d}\left(M_{N_{1},E}^{2}(\theta)\right)\|-\hat{L}^{d}\right|>\varepsilon\cdot\hat{L}^{d}\right\}\Big)\leq\frac{1}{20}

Proof.

By Lemma
2.2
, we can choose
N_{0}:=N_{0}(\varepsilon,\alpha,E,d)
so that for
n\geq N_{0}
, one has
\mathrm{mes}(\mathcal{B}_{n,E,\varepsilon}^{d})\leq e^{-n\varepsilon^{2}\cdot\hat{L}^{d}(d\alpha,M_{E})}
and, furthermore,

(5.429)

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}e^{-2^{j}c_{1}\varepsilon^{2}N_{0}\cdot\hat{L}^{d}(d\alpha,M_{E})}<1/100,

where
c_{1}
is the constant in Lemma
2.2
,
as well as

(5.430)

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}e^{-2^{j+1}N_{0}(1-\varepsilon)\hat{L}^{d}(d\alpha,M_{E})}\cdot e^{2^{j+5}N_{0}\varepsilon\hat{L}^{d}(d\alpha,M_{E})}\cdot e^{8\tilde{C}_{0}c_{0}}<c_{0},

where
\tilde{C}_{0},c_{0}>0
are the constants in Theorem
2.4
.
For the purposes of this proof, we let
\mathcal{G}_{n}:=(\mathcal{B}_{n,E,\varepsilon}^{d})^{c}
, omitting the fixed parameters
E
and
\varepsilon
for simplicity.
We also denote
\hat{L}^{k}(d\alpha,M_{E})
by
\hat{L}^{k}
.
First, we need the following estimate at the fixed scale
N_{0}
.

Lemma 5.4
.

There exists
\kappa_{0}=\kappa_{0}(\varepsilon,\alpha,E,d,N_{0})
such that for any
\|\kappa\|_{\mathbb{T}}\leq\kappa_{0}
, and for any
\theta_{0}
satisfying

(5.431)

\displaystyle\|\wedge^{d}M_{2N_{0},E}(\theta_{0})\|\geq e^{2N_{0}(1-\varepsilon)\hat{L}^{d}}

one has the bound

(5.432)

\displaystyle\|\wedge^{d}\left(M_{N_{0},E}(\theta_{0}+N_{0}d\alpha+\kappa)M_{N_{0},E}(\theta_{0})\right)\|\geq e^{2N_{0}(1-2\varepsilon)\hat{L}^{d}}.

Proof.

We use the following estimate from
[
DK
]
:

Lemma 5.5
.

[
DK
]
*Lemma 2.8
For
k\times k
complex matrices
h_{1},h_{2}
, for any
1\leq j\leq k
,

(5.433)

\displaystyle\|\wedge^{j}h_{1}-\wedge^{j}h_{2}\|\leq j\max\{1,\|h_{1}\|,\|h_{2}\|\}^{j-1}\,\|h_{1}-h_{2}\|.

We apply Lemma
5.5
to
k=2d
,
j=d
, and

(5.434)

\displaystyle h_{1}(\theta)=

\displaystyle M_{N_{0},E}(\theta+N_{0}d\alpha+\kappa)M_{N_{0},E}(\theta)

(5.435)

\displaystyle h_{2}(\theta)=

\displaystyle M_{N_{0},E}(\theta+N_{0}d\alpha)M_{N_{0},E}(\theta)

By Lemma
2.1
, uniformly in
\theta
,

(5.436)

\displaystyle\max(\|h_{1}(\theta)\|,\|h_{2}(\theta)\|)\leq e^{2N_{0}(1+\varepsilon)\hat{L}^{d}},\text{ and }\|M_{N_{0},E}(\theta)\|\leq e^{N_{0}(1+\varepsilon)\hat{L}^{d}}.

Hence, Lemma
5.5
implies furthermore that uniformly in
\theta
,

(5.437)

\displaystyle\|\wedge^{d}h_{1}(\theta)-\wedge^{d}h_{2}(\theta)\|\leq

\displaystyle de^{2dN_{0}(1+\varepsilon)\hat{L}^{d}}\cdot\|h_{1}(\theta)-h_{2}(\theta)\|

(5.438)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle de^{3dN_{0}(1+\varepsilon)\hat{L}^{d}}\cdot\|M_{N_{0},E}(\theta+N_{0}d\alpha+\kappa)-M_{N_{0},E}(\theta+N_{0}d\alpha)\|

(5.439)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{\alpha,d,E,N_{0}}e^{4dN_{0}(1+\varepsilon)\hat{L}^{d}}\cdot\|\kappa\|_{\mathbb{T}},

where in the last step we used a telescoping argument.
Hence for
\|\kappa\|_{\mathbb{T}}\leq\kappa_{0}(\varepsilon,\alpha,E,d,N_{0})
, one has uniformly in
\theta
,

(5.440)

\displaystyle\|\wedge^{d}h_{1}(\theta)-\wedge^{d}h_{2}(\theta)\|\leq e^{2N_{0}(1-2\varepsilon)\hat{L}^{d}}.

This, combined with (
5.431
) at
\theta=\theta_{0}
, implies that

(5.441)

\displaystyle\|\wedge^{d}(M_{N_{0},E}(\theta_{0}+N_{0}d\alpha+\kappa)M_{N_{0},E}(\theta_{0}))\|\geq e^{2N_{0}(1-\varepsilon)\hat{L}^{d}}-e^{2N_{0}(1-2\varepsilon)\hat{L}^{d}}\geq e^{2N_{0}(1-2\varepsilon)\hat{L}^{d}},

which is the claimed result.
∎

Next, we pick an arbitrary
\kappa_{0}
-admissible
N_{1}
(i.e.,
\|N_{1}d\alpha\|_{\mathbb{T}}\leq\kappa_{0}
), satisfying
N_{1}\geq 8N_{0}
.
Let
k\geq 2
be such that
2^{k+1}N_{0}\leq N_{1}<2^{k+2}N_{0}
, and
n_{0}=N_{1}-2^{k}N_{0}=N_{1}-N_{0}-\sum_{\ell=0}^{k-1}2^{\ell}N_{0}
be the remainder.
Clearly

(5.442)

\displaystyle 2^{k}N_{0}\leq n_{0}\leq(3\cdot 2^{k}-1)N_{0}.

Our goal is to estimate

(5.443)

\displaystyle\log\|\wedge^{d}(M_{N_{1},E}^{2}(\theta))\|=\log\|\wedge^{d}(M_{N_{1},E}(\theta+N_{1}d\alpha+\kappa)M_{N_{1},E}(\theta))\|,

where
\kappa=-N_{1}d\alpha
(modulo
\mathbb{Z}
) satisfies
\|\kappa\|_{\mathbb{T}}\leq\kappa_{0}
.
In order to accomplish this, we decompose

(5.444)

\displaystyle M_{N_{1},E}(\theta+N_{1}d\alpha+\kappa)M_{N_{1},E}(\theta)=M_{N_{1},E}(\theta)^{2}

(5.445)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle M_{n_{0},E}(\theta+(2N_{1}-n_{0})d\alpha+\kappa)M_{2^{k-1}N_{0},E}(\theta+(N_{1}+2^{k-1}N_{0})d\alpha+\kappa)\cdots

(5.446)

\displaystyle\cdots M_{4N_{0},E}(\theta+(N_{1}+2^{2}N_{0})d\alpha+\kappa)M_{2N_{0},E}(\theta+(N_{1}+2N_{0})d\alpha+\kappa)

(5.447)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\cdot M_{N_{0},E}(\theta+(N_{1}+N_{0})d\alpha+\kappa)M_{N_{0},E}(\theta+N_{1}d\alpha+\kappa)\cdot

(5.448)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\cdot M_{N_{0},E}(\theta+(N_{1}-N_{0})d\alpha)M_{N_{0},E}(\theta+(N_{1}-2N_{0})d\alpha)

(5.449)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\cdot M_{2N_{0},E}(\theta+(N_{1}-2^{2}N_{0})d\alpha)M_{4N_{0},E}(\theta+(N_{1}-2^{3}N_{0})d\alpha)

(5.450)

\displaystyle\cdots M_{2^{k-1}N_{0},E}(\theta+n_{0}d\alpha)M_{n_{0},E}(\theta)

Step 1
.
Let

(5.451)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{N_{0}}^{(1)}:=

\displaystyle(\mathcal{G}_{N_{0}}-(N_{1}-2N_{0})d\alpha)\cap(\mathcal{G}_{N_{0}}-(N_{1}-N_{0})d\alpha)\cap(\mathcal{G}_{N_{0}}-N_{1}d\alpha-\kappa)\cap

(5.452)

\displaystyle\cap(\mathcal{G}_{N_{0}}-(N_{1}+N_{0})d\alpha-\kappa)\cap(\mathcal{G}_{2N_{0}}-(N_{1}-2N_{0})d\alpha)\cap(\mathcal{G}_{2N_{0}}-N_{1}d\alpha-\kappa)\cap

(5.453)

\displaystyle\cap(\mathcal{G}_{2N_{0}}-(N_{1}-N_{0})d\alpha)

We restrict to
\theta\in\mathcal{G}_{N_{0}}^{(1)}
for the remainder of the proof.
Let

(5.454)

\displaystyle g_{N_{0},1}(\theta)=

\displaystyle\wedge^{d}M_{N_{0},E}(\theta+(N_{1}-2N_{0})d\alpha)

(5.455)

\displaystyle g_{N_{0},2}(\theta)=

\displaystyle\wedge^{d}M_{N_{0},E}(\theta+(N_{1}-N_{0})d\alpha)

(5.456)

\displaystyle g_{N_{0},3}(\theta)=

\displaystyle\wedge^{d}M_{N_{0},E}(\theta+N_{1}d\alpha+\kappa)

(5.457)

\displaystyle g_{N_{0},4}(\theta)=

\displaystyle\wedge^{d}M_{N_{0},E}(\theta+(N_{1}+N_{0})d\alpha+\kappa)

The goal of Step 1 is to show the following.

Lemma 5.6
.

For any
\theta\in\mathcal{G}_{N_{0}}^{(1)}
, one has

(5.458)

\displaystyle\log\|g_{N_{0},4}(\theta)\cdots g_{N_{0},1}(\theta)\|\geq 4N_{0}(1-3\varepsilon)\hat{L}^{d}-4\tilde{C}_{0}\tilde{\kappa}_{N_{0}}\cdot\tilde{\varepsilon}_{N_{0}}^{-2},

for the constant
\tilde{C}_{0}>0
in Theorem
2.4
and with

(5.459)

\displaystyle\tilde{\kappa}_{N_{0}}=e^{-2N_{0}(1-\varepsilon)\hat{L}_{d}+2^{2}N_{0}\varepsilon\hat{L}^{d-1}},\text{ and }\tilde{\varepsilon}_{N_{0}}=e^{-2^{3}N_{0}\varepsilon\hat{L}^{d}}.

Furthermore,

(5.460)

\displaystyle\mathrm{mes}(\mathcal{G}_{N_{0}}^{(1)})\geq 1-4e^{-c_{1}\varepsilon^{2}N_{0}\hat{L}^{d}}-3e^{-2c_{1}\varepsilon^{2}N_{0}\hat{L}^{d}},

where
c_{1}>0
is the constant in Lemma
2.2
.

Proof.

By Lemma
2.2
, for all
\theta\in\mathcal{G}_{N_{0}}^{(1)}
, we have

(5.461)

\displaystyle e^{N_{0}(1-\varepsilon)\hat{L}^{d}}\leq\sigma_{1}(g_{N_{0},j}(\theta))\leq e^{N_{0}(1+\varepsilon)\hat{L}^{d}},\text{ for }j=1,2,3,4,

and

(5.462)

\displaystyle\|g_{N_{0},4}(\theta)g_{N_{0},3}(\theta)\|\geq e^{2N_{0}(1-\varepsilon)\hat{L}^{d}},\text{ and }\|g_{N_{0},2}(\theta)g_{N_{0},1}(\theta)\|\geq e^{2N_{0}(1-\varepsilon)\hat{L}^{d}}.

Note that by definition
\theta\in\mathcal{G}_{N_{0}}^{(1)}\subset\mathcal{G}_{2N_{0}}-(N_{1}-N_{0})d\alpha
, hence

(5.463)

\displaystyle\|\wedge^{d}(M_{N_{0},E}(\theta+N_{1}d\alpha)M_{N_{0},E}(\theta+(N_{1}-N_{0})d\alpha))\|\geq e^{2N_{0}(1-\varepsilon)\hat{L}^{d}}.

Combining the above (which verifies the assumptions of Lemma
5.4
for
\theta_{0}=\theta+(N_{1}-N_{0})d\alpha
) with Lemma
5.4
and
\|\kappa\|_{\mathbb{T}}\leq\kappa_{0}
, yields

(5.464)

\displaystyle\|g_{N_{0},3}(\theta)g_{N_{0},2}(\theta)\|=

\displaystyle\|\wedge^{d}(M_{N_{0},E}(\theta+N_{1}d\alpha+\kappa)M_{N_{0},E}(\theta+(N_{1}-N_{0})d\alpha))\|

(5.465)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle e^{2N_{0}(1-2\varepsilon)\hat{L}^{d}}.

By Lemma
2.1
applied to the matrix
\wedge^{2}(\wedge^{d}M_{N_{0},E})
and

(5.466)

\displaystyle{L}^{1}(d\alpha,\wedge^{2}(\wedge^{d}M_{E}))=

\displaystyle{L}^{1}(d\alpha,\wedge^{d}M_{E})+{L}^{2}(d\alpha,\wedge^{d}M_{E})

(5.467)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{L}^{d}+{L}^{d-1}+{L}_{d+1}=2{L}^{d-1},

one has uniformly in
\theta\in\mathbb{T}
,

(5.468)

\displaystyle\sigma_{1}(\wedge^{d}M_{N_{0},E}(\theta))\cdot\sigma_{2}(\wedge^{d}M_{N_{0},E}(\theta))=\sigma_{1}(\wedge^{2}(\wedge^{d}M_{N_{0},E}(\theta)))\leq e^{2N_{0}(1+\varepsilon)\hat{L}^{d-1}}.

Combining the above with the lower bounds in (
5.461
), we conclude that

(5.469)

\displaystyle\sigma_{2}(g_{N_{0},j}(\theta))\leq e^{N_{0}(1-\varepsilon)(\hat{L}^{d-1}+\hat{L}_{d+1})}\cdot e^{4N_{0}\varepsilon\hat{L}^{d-1}}.

Note that (
5.461
), (
5.469
), (
5.462
) and (
5.464
) verify the assumptions of Theorem
2.4
, the Avalanche Principle. In fact, for
j=1,2,3,4
,

(5.470)

\displaystyle\frac{\sigma_{1}(g_{N_{0},j}(\theta))}{\sigma_{2}(g_{N_{0},j}(\theta))}\geq e^{2N_{0}(1-\varepsilon)\hat{L}_{d}}e^{-4N_{0}\varepsilon\hat{L}^{d-1}}=:\tilde{\kappa}_{N_{0}}^{-1},

and for
j=1,2,3
,

(5.471)

\displaystyle\frac{\|g_{N_{0},j+1}(\theta)g_{N_{0},j}(\theta)\|}{\sigma_{1}(g_{N_{0},j+1}(\theta))\cdot\sigma_{1}(g_{N_{0},j}(\theta))}\geq e^{-2^{3}N_{0}\varepsilon\hat{L}^{d}}=:\tilde{\varepsilon}_{N_{0}}.

Clearly,

(5.472)

\displaystyle\tilde{\kappa}_{N_{0}}\cdot\tilde{\varepsilon}_{N_{0}}^{-2}\leq e^{-2N_{0}(1-\varepsilon)\hat{L}_{d}}e^{(2^{4}+2^{2})N_{0}\varepsilon\hat{L}^{d}}<c_{0},

due to (
5.430
).
Therefore, by applying Theorem
2.4
to
g_{N_{0},4}(\theta)\cdots g_{N_{0},1}(\theta)
, we obtain with the constant
\tilde{C}_{0}>0
in Theorem
2.4
,

(5.473)

\displaystyle\log\|g_{N_{0},4}(\theta)\cdots g_{N_{0},1}(\theta)\|\geq

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{3}\log\|g_{N_{0},j+1}(\theta)g_{N_{0},j}(\theta)\|-\sum_{j=2}^{3}\log\|g_{N_{0},j}(\theta)\|-4\tilde{C}_{0}\tilde{\kappa}_{N_{0}}\cdot\tilde{\varepsilon}_{N_{0}}^{-2}

(5.474)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle 4N_{0}(1-3\varepsilon)\hat{L}^{d}-4\tilde{C}_{0}\tilde{\kappa}_{N_{0}}\cdot\tilde{\varepsilon}_{N_{0}}^{-2}.

Regarding the measure estimate, we note that

(5.475)

\displaystyle\mathrm{mes}(\mathcal{G}_{N_{0}}^{(1)})\geq 1-4e^{-c_{1}\varepsilon^{2}N_{0}\hat{L}^{d}}-3e^{-2c_{1}\varepsilon^{2}N_{0}\hat{L}^{d}},

where we used that for any
n\geq N_{0}
,
\mathrm{mes}(\mathcal{G}_{n})=1-\mathrm{mes}(\mathcal{B}_{n,E,\varepsilon}^{d})\geq 1-e^{-c_{1}\varepsilon^{2}n\hat{L}^{d}}
, due to Lemma
2.2
.
∎

To better illustrate the inductive procedure, we present the second step in detail as well.

Step 2
. Let

(5.476)

\displaystyle g_{2N_{0},1}(\theta)=

\displaystyle\wedge^{d}M_{2N_{0},E}(\theta+(N_{1}-2^{2}N_{0})d\alpha)

(5.477)

\displaystyle g_{2N_{0},2}(\theta)=

\displaystyle\wedge^{d}M_{2N_{0},E}(\theta+(N_{1}-2N_{0})d\alpha)

(5.478)

\displaystyle g_{2N_{0},3}(\theta)=

\displaystyle\wedge^{d}M_{2N_{0},E}(\theta+N_{1}d\alpha+\kappa)

(5.479)

\displaystyle g_{2N_{0},4}(\theta)=

\displaystyle\wedge^{d}M_{2N_{0},E}(\theta+(N_{1}+2N_{0})d\alpha+\kappa)

Note that

g_{2N_{0},3}(\theta)g_{2N_{0},2}(\theta)=g_{N_{0},4}(\theta)\cdots g_{N_{0},1}(\theta).

Hence by Lemma
5.6
, for
\theta\in\mathcal{G}_{N_{0}}^{(1)}
,

(5.480)

\displaystyle\log\|g_{2N_{0},3}(\theta)g_{2N_{0},2}(\theta)\|\geq 4N_{0}(1-3\varepsilon)\hat{L}^{d}-4\tilde{C}_{0}\tilde{\kappa}_{N_{0}}\cdot\tilde{\varepsilon}_{N_{0}}^{-2}.

We further restrict
\theta
to the following subset of
\mathcal{G}_{N_{0}}^{(1)}
:

(5.481)

\displaystyle\theta\in\mathcal{G}_{2N_{0}}^{(1)}=

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{N_{0}}^{(1)}\cap(\mathcal{G}_{2N_{0}}-(N_{1}-2^{2}N_{0})d\alpha)\cap(\mathcal{G}_{2N_{0}}-(N_{1}+2N_{0})d\alpha-\kappa)\cap

(5.482)

\displaystyle\qquad\cap(\mathcal{G}_{4N_{0}}-(N_{1}-2^{2}N_{0})d\alpha)\cap(\mathcal{G}_{4N_{0}}-N_{1}d\alpha-\kappa).

Our goal of Step 2 is to prove the following:

Lemma 5.7
.

For
\theta\in\mathcal{G}_{2N_{0}}^{(1)}
,

(5.483)

\displaystyle\log\|g_{2N_{0},4}(\theta)\cdots g_{2N_{0},1}(\theta)\|\geq 2^{3}N_{0}(1-3\varepsilon)\hat{L}^{d}-4\tilde{C}_{0}\sum_{\ell=0}^{1}\tilde{\kappa}_{2^{\ell}N_{0}}\cdot\tilde{\varepsilon}_{2^{\ell}N_{0}}^{-2},

where

(5.484)

\displaystyle\tilde{\kappa}_{2N_{0}}=e^{-2^{2}N_{0}(1-\varepsilon)\hat{L}_{d}}\cdot e^{2^{3}N_{0}\varepsilon\hat{L}^{d-1}}\,\text{ and }\tilde{\varepsilon}_{2N_{0}}=e^{-2^{4}N_{0}\varepsilon\hat{L}^{d}}\cdot e^{-4\tilde{C}_{0}\tilde{\kappa}_{N_{0}}\cdot\tilde{\varepsilon}_{N_{0}}^{-2}}.

Furthermore,

(5.485)

\displaystyle\mathrm{mes}(G_{2N_{0}}^{(1)})\geq 1-4e^{-c_{1}\varepsilon^{2}N_{0}\hat{L}^{d}}-5e^{-2c_{1}\varepsilon^{2}N_{0}\hat{L}^{d}}-2e^{-4c_{1}\varepsilon^{2}N_{0}\hat{L}^{d}}.

Proof.

Since
\theta\in\mathcal{G}_{2N_{0}}^{(1)}
, by Lemma
2.2
one has for
j=1,2,3,4
that

(5.486)

\displaystyle e^{2N_{0}(1-\varepsilon)\hat{L}^{d}}\leq\sigma_{1}(g_{2N_{0},j}(\theta))\leq e^{2N_{0}(1-\varepsilon)\hat{L}^{d}},

and for
j=1,3
that

(5.487)

\displaystyle\|g_{2N_{0},j+1}(\theta)g_{2N_{0},j}(\theta)\|\geq e^{4N_{0}(1-\varepsilon)\hat{L}^{d}}.

In analogy to (
5.469
), we have for
j=1,2,3,4
that

(5.488)

\displaystyle\sigma_{2}(g_{2N_{0},j}(\theta))\leq e^{2N_{0}(1-\varepsilon)(\hat{L}^{d-1}+\hat{L}_{d+1})}\cdot e^{8N_{0}\varepsilon\hat{L}^{d-1}}.

We apply the avalanche principle (AP) Theorem
2.4
to
g_{2N_{0},4}(\theta),...,g_{2N_{0},1}(\theta)
. The hypotheses of the AP hold since:
for
j=1,2,3,4
, by (
5.486
) and (
5.488
),

(5.489)

\displaystyle\frac{\sigma_{1}(g_{2N_{0},j}(\theta))}{\sigma_{2}(g_{2N_{0},j}(\theta))}\geq e^{2^{2}N_{0}(1-\varepsilon)\hat{L}_{d}}e^{-2^{3}N_{0}\varepsilon\hat{L}^{d-1}}=:\tilde{\kappa}_{2N_{0}}^{-1},

and for
j=1,2,3
, by further taking (
5.487
) and (
5.480
) into account,

(5.490)

\displaystyle\frac{\|g_{2N_{0},j+1}(\theta)g_{2N_{0},j}(\theta)\|}{\sigma_{1}(g_{2N_{0},j+1}(\theta))\cdot\sigma_{1}(g_{2N_{0},j}(\theta))}\geq e^{-2^{4}N_{0}\varepsilon\hat{L}^{d}}e^{-4\tilde{C}_{0}\tilde{\kappa}_{N_{0}}\cdot\tilde{\varepsilon}_{N_{0}}^{-2}}=:\tilde{\varepsilon}_{2N_{0}}.

Clearly,

(5.491)

\displaystyle\tilde{\kappa}_{2N_{0}}\cdot\tilde{\varepsilon}_{2N_{0}}^{-2}\leq e^{-2^{2}N_{0}(1-\varepsilon)\hat{L}_{d}}e^{(2^{5}+2^{3})N_{0}\varepsilon\hat{L}^{d}}e^{8\tilde{C}_{0}c_{0}}<c_{0},

where we used (
5.472
) to bound
\tilde{\kappa}_{N_{0}}\cdot\tilde{\varepsilon}_{N_{0}}^{-2}<c_{0}
.
Therefore, Theorem
2.4
implies that

(5.492)

\displaystyle\log\|g_{2N_{0},4}(\theta)\cdots g_{2N_{0},1}(\theta)\|\geq

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{3}\log\|g_{2N_{0},j+1}(\theta)g_{2N_{0},j}(\theta)\|-\sum_{j=2}^{3}\log\|g_{2N_{0},j}(\theta)\|-4\tilde{C}_{0}\tilde{\kappa}_{2N_{0}}\cdot\tilde{\varepsilon}_{2N_{0}}^{-2}

(5.493)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle 2^{3}N_{0}(1-3\varepsilon)\hat{L}^{d}-4\tilde{C}_{0}\sum_{\ell=0}^{1}\tilde{\kappa}_{2^{\ell}N_{0}}\cdot\tilde{\varepsilon}_{2^{\ell}N_{0}}^{-2}.

Regarding the measure estimate, Lemma
2.2
implies that

(5.494)

\displaystyle\mathrm{mes}(\mathcal{G}_{2N_{0}}^{(1)})\geq

\displaystyle\mathrm{mes}(\mathcal{G}_{N_{0}}^{(1)})-2e^{-2c_{1}\varepsilon^{2}N_{0}}-2e^{-2c_{2}\varepsilon^{2}N_{0}}

(5.495)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle 1-4e^{-c_{1}\varepsilon^{2}N_{0}\hat{L}^{d}}-5e^{-2c_{1}\varepsilon^{2}N_{0}\hat{L}^{d}}-2e^{-4c_{1}\varepsilon^{2}N_{0}\hat{L}^{d}}.

This is the claimed result.
∎

\displaystyle e^{-2^{\ell+3}N_{0}\varepsilon\hat{L}^{d}}\cdot e^{-4\tilde{C}_{0}\sum_{r=0}^{\ell-1}\tilde{\kappa}_{2^{r}N_{0}}\cdot\tilde{\varepsilon}_{2^{r}N_{0}}^{-2}}.

Note that these definitions are consistent with those in (

) for

(5.503)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell=0}^{n}e^{-2^{\ell+1}N_{0}(1-\varepsilon)\hat{L}_{d}}\cdot e^{(2^{\ell+4}+2^{\ell+2})N_{0}\varepsilon\hat{L}^{d}}\cdot e^{8\tilde{C}_{0}c_{0}}<c_{0},

by (
5.430
), (
5.472
), and induction.

Step

.

.

, one has

\displaystyle\log\|g_{2^{m-1}N_{0},4}(\theta)\cdots g_{2^{m-1}N_{0},1}(\theta)\|\geq 2^{m+1}N_{0}(1-3\varepsilon)\hat{L}^{d}-4\tilde{C}_{0}\sum_{\ell=0}^{m-1}\tilde{\kappa}_{2^{\ell}N_{0}}\cdot\tilde{\varepsilon}_{2^{\ell}N_{0}}^{-2}.

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{2^{m-1}N_{0}}^{(1)}\subset(\mathcal{G}_{2^{m}N_{0}}-(N_{1}-2^{m}N_{0})d\alpha)\cap(\mathcal{G}_{2^{m}N_{0}}-N_{1}d\alpha-\kappa)

\theta\in\mathcal{G}_{2^{m-1}N_{0}}^{(1)}
, Lemma

implies for

that

to

), (

) and (

) we have that for

\displaystyle\frac{\|g_{2^{m-1}N_{0},j+1}(\theta)g_{2^{m-1}N_{0},j}(\theta)\|}{\sigma_{1}(g_{2^{m-1}N_{0},j+1}(\theta))\cdot\sigma_{1}(g_{2^{m-1}N_{0},j}(\theta))}\geq e^{-2^{m+2}N_{0}\varepsilon\hat{L}^{d}}\cdot e^{-4\tilde{C}_{0}\sum_{\ell=0}^{m-2}\tilde{\kappa}_{2^{\ell}N_{0}}\cdot\tilde{\varepsilon}_{2^{\ell}N_{0}}^{-2}}=\tilde{\varepsilon}_{2^{m-1}N_{0}}.

Clearly,

(5.519)

\displaystyle\tilde{\kappa}_{2^{m-1}N_{0}}\cdot\tilde{\varepsilon}_{2^{m-1}N_{0}}^{-2}\leq

\displaystyle e^{-2^{m}N_{0}(1-\varepsilon)\hat{L}_{d}}\cdot e^{(2^{m+3}+2^{m+1})N_{0}\varepsilon\hat{L}^{d}}\cdot e^{8\tilde{C}_{0}\sum_{\ell=0}^{m-2}\tilde{\kappa}_{2^{\ell}N_{0}}\cdot\tilde{\varepsilon}_{2^{\ell}N_{0}}^{-2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle e^{-2^{m}N_{0}(1-\varepsilon)\hat{L}_{d}}\cdot e^{(2^{m+3}+2^{m+1})N_{0}\varepsilon\hat{L}^{d}}\cdot e^{8\tilde{C}_{0}c_{0}}<c_{0},

where we used (
5.502
). Therefore, Theorem

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-4\tilde{C}_{0}\tilde{\kappa}_{2^{m-1}N_{0}}\cdot\tilde{\varepsilon}_{2^{m-1}N_{0}}^{-2}

∎
